# Coda — A Note on Method and Sources

A book that predicts deserves to have its machinery inspected, and
this note exists so that the inspection need not be forensic. It
states what kind of claim the book makes, how the evidence was
tiered, what rules governed the comparisons, what the book does not
claim, and where the argument's founder was right and is respectfully
departed from.

## I. Mechanism under conditions, not determinism

The book's causal claim has a precise logical form, and everything
turns on it: a *mechanism under conditions*, not a law of history.
The mechanism — capital-intensification of decisive force compelling
permanent finance, permanent finance compelling administrative
capacity, administrative capacity subordinating intermediate
authorities — operated once, in the documented case Part II
reconstructs. The book's wager is that its enabling conditions have
recurred: a decisive capability whose establishment cost sits beyond
what any subject can sustain; polities with latent fiscal-administrative capacity
to mobilise; and exogenous pressure sufficient to force the
mobilisation. Where the conditions fail, the mechanism idles — the
book's own counter-cases are the demonstration, and they were chosen
for that work: the Swiss won the battles and built no state, because
pike squares compelled no institutions; the Empire asserted the
monopoly of justice without the fiscal substrate and enforced it a
century late; Burgundy built the apparatus and lost the continuous
political centre, and the apparatus passed to its neighbours; England
held the gun monopoly without the fisc and lost both. One mechanism,
several failure modes, one completed run — that is the shape of a
conditional claim, and it is why the predictions of Part III are
conditional too: *if* the stacks continue to concentrate, *if* the
blocs sustain the fiscal effort, *then* the settlement pattern
follows. History does not repeat on command. Prices, however, are
facts, and the book's core empirical assertion — that the price of
decisive capability has moved beyond subordinate reach — is checkable
at any time against public budgets.

## II. The comparative register, stated as rule

No single-polity argument appears anywhere in the book; the rule was
doctrine before a word was drafted, and it is auditable. Every
English-leaning chapter carries at least two continental comparators:
Henry VI is read beside Charles VI and the Ottoman succession crises;
Cade beside the Praguerie, the Ciompi legacy and the German risings;
the Pastons beside the *Fehde* and the vendetta; the artillery state
is French, with Burgundy and the Swiss as counter-cases and England
demoted to the control experiment; the new monarchies run four
polities with the Ottomans included precisely because the older "New
Monarchy" literature excluded them. Appendix B tabulates the register
chapter by chapter. The rule's purpose is not decorative breadth. It
is the book's defence against its own convenience: a mechanism that
only worked in one kingdom would be a national history wearing a
theory; a mechanism whose failure modes appear exactly where its
conditions fail is a mechanism.

## III. The three-tier sourcing discipline

The book runs on three distinct bodies of evidence, and they were
never allowed to vouch for one another.

The *neomedieval spine* — Bull, Friedrichs, Cerny, Kobrin, Slaughter,
Kaplan, Williams, McFate — was read as a literature being extended,
and quoted from its own texts wherever they were in hand, with every
second-hand quotation named as such — and retired when the primary
arrived: Bull's caution, carried through named intermediaries for
the whole research phase with one word flagged as varying by
printing, was re-quoted direct from the fourth edition when the
book reached the corpus, and the variant proved to be two adjacent
sentences on one page. The *medieval historiography* was held to the hardest
rule in the book: no gated work cited as if consulted; every claim
graded by tier; omission preferred to invention. Where the research
corrected the project's own working assumptions, the corrections are
in the text: the "sixty fortified places" of the early outline
became Rogers's sourced one hundred strongpoints with the
surrender-not-siege caveat that improved the argument; the Bureau
brothers were demoted from inventors to institutionalisers, which
strengthened the thesis; Formigny was downgraded from decision to
provocation; the drone-share figures of the last chapters are kept
as three separate measurements because that is what they are. And
the *contemporary record* was held to primary documents — statutes,
regulations, court filings, proxy statements, budget lines, the
institutions' own annual reports — with commentary as framing,
never as load-bearing support; every live episode is drafted as
dated events, attributed, and flagged for re-verification at press.
Two special disciplines ran across all three tiers. Foreign-language
sources are quoted verbatim in the original, with translations
either the scholarly edition's or marked as the pipeline's own for
checking. And a recurring evidentiary pattern was promoted, once
noticed, into the book's method: at every contested joint of the
medieval mechanism, the argument rests on the *sceptics'* own
concessions — Rogers's spiral, DeVries's conforming France and
Burgundy, Curry's state-only threshold, Cerny's fifteenth-century
attribution — so that what remains in dispute with the specialists
is ranking, tempo and sequence, never direction.

One disclosure belongs in this note as a matter of the same
discipline. The research and drafting pipeline for this book used
AI models, including Anthropic's; the Anthropic–Pentagon episode
discussed in Chapters 3 and 11 entered the book on documentary
criteria — government releases and docket entries — and carries the
standard live-event flag, like every other contemporary episode.
The reader should weigh the book's treatment of that episode with
the disclosure in view; the sourcing rules above were built so that
nothing in the treatment depends on trust.

## IV. The wager's narrowing — a delta table

The falsifiability passage in the Introduction is not the wager the
project began with; it is the wager the research left standing. The
narrowing was always in one direction — each condition tightened,
none loosened — and the method essay is the place to show the
deltas, because the direction of revision is itself evidence of how
the book was built.

| # | The outline's original condition | The narrowed condition as published | What forced the narrowing |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Cheap distributed autonomy defeats concentrated capital | A full-stack power defeated by cheap distributed systems *fielded without a patron's intelligence, connectivity and interceptor layers behind them* | The Ukraine record: the March 2025 suspension week showed the cheap layer aiming through a patron's decisive layer (ch12) |
| 2 | Privacy-preserving systems become the default | Bloc-internal fiscal and reporting reach *falls in fact*; the offshore untaxed share *rises again*; plural privacy-default stacks displace bloc stacks *as the operative default* | The legibility record: the offshore stock persists while the untaxed fraction collapsed threefold — the honest baseline required a sharper falsifier (ch08) |
| 3 | Platforms consolidate independence | An actor with decisive-relevant capability and a majority-commercial fiscal base escapes the state's instruments *and resolves its disputes with states in fora states do not control* | The legal history: plural fora proved parasitic on state law; forum-migration is the discriminating test (chs. 8, 11) |
| 4 | No identifiable Tudor moves | The five moves decomposed into scoreable forms across all four candidates, *plus* the Depreter limb: canon alone consolidating a bloc would falsify the mechanism even while producing a settlement look-alike | The comparative work (ch11) and the Burgundian accounts (ch10): apparatus without a continuous centre fails — so the EU case must be able to cut against the book |

A wager that tightens itself under research is behaving as a wager
should; a wager that loosens is becoming unfalsifiable. The reader
is invited to hold the book to the published version and to none
softer.

## V. What the book does not claim

Four disclaimers, stated flatly because each has been earned by a
chapter. The book does not predict specific events — no dates for
settlements, no named winners of elections or wars; its unit of
prediction is the pattern and its horizon is fifteen years of named
indicators. It does not claim any polity's success: the American
candidacy is called probable and half-conscious, the Chinese
furthest along but exposed at the summit the book's own
two-bodies chapter diagnoses, the European conditional on a single
fisc that does not yet exist, the Indian possible; all four
could fail, and condition four of the wager scores exactly that. It
does not claim the settlement is desirable: the Tudor turn, where
it ran, ran through bonds, expulsions, dissolutions and a harvest
of heads, and its modern forms — whatever their legality — will
concentrate power in ways the book's readers may reasonably dread;
the book predicts, and declines to bless. And it does not claim
that history repeats: it claims that a mechanism has recurred, and
it stakes the claim on measurements.

One limit is confessed rather than disclaimed, because it is a
question the book can pose and cannot answer. The consolidations
this book predicts run beneath a nuclear overhang whose top layer,
the decade's reporting suggests, is entangled with the decisive
stack itself: to fight the full transparency war between blocs
would mean striking systems that also run the adversary's
deterrent, and nobody professes to know where the red lines lie —
only that they exist (Chapter 12 carries the point with its
source). If that war is unfightable, the blocs may consolidate and
never test one another: a permanent, armed, mutually observed
pause rather than a Bosworth. Whether such a pause is a Tudor
settlement — a settlement being, in the end, an order nobody finds
it worth fighting — or something without precedent in the
mechanism's history, this book does not know, and says so. The
wager's scoring does not depend on the answer: the indicators
measure consolidation within blocs, and a pause between them
would leave every one of them scoreable.

## VI. The debt to Bull, and the precise disagreement

The debt first, because it is total: without the tenth chapter of
*The Anarchical Society* there is no literature for this book to
extend, and without Bull's own scepticism there is no model for how
to hold a bold hypothesis honestly. Bull did the two things this
book has tried to imitate — he specified the alternative to the
states system with enough precision to test, and he then declined
to claim it on the evidence of his decade. The disagreement is
correspondingly precise. It is not about the diagnosis: on the
evidence assembled in Part I, Bull's five trends have matured past
his own threshold, and the book's first chapter argues that his
caution, right in 1977, would be wrong now. It is about the
*stability* of the condition the diagnosis names. Bull entertained
the new medievalism as a possible successor system — a durable
alternative form of universal political organisation. The book's
history says otherwise: the first medieval order was durable for
centuries precisely because no actor could carry the cost of locking
it shut, and it became irreversible within two generations of that
cost becoming payable. A dispersal is not a settlement; it is what a settlement
looks like before the bill for decisive force arrives. Where Bull
saw a possible destination, this book sees a waypoint with a
well-documented exit — and it has tried to honour him in the only
way that matters, by saying exactly what evidence would prove the
dispersal stable after all, and publishing the list.


---

# Appendix A — The Neomedievalism Literature, Annotated

## I. The founding hypothesis

**Hedley Bull, *The Anarchical Society*, 4th ed. (Columbia UP, 2012;
first ed. 1977), chs. 10–12.** *[T2 — IN REPO, read first-hand]* The
founding "new mediaevalism" hypothesis (ch. 10, p. 245: "a system of
overlapping authority and multiple loyalty"); the five prima facie
trends, examined in **ch. 11, "The Decline of the States System?"
(pp. 248–66)** under numbered headings — a structure the secondary
literature's "ch. 10" citations flattened; and Bull's verdict, verbatim
at p. 265 ("no clear evidence that in the next few decades the states
system is likely to give place…"), with the "giving way" sentence
adjacent — the way/place variance among intermediaries dissolved on
the page. His per-trend tests (reproduce-vs-replace; re-subordination
of the multinationals; interaction without unity of outlook) are
flagged as a [DELTA] to the Ch1 argument. Engaged: Introduction, Ch1,
the Coda. Pagination matches the 2002 printing; bibliography cites the
4th ed.; the scan's OCR is rough — re-verify further verbatim against
page images.

## II. Theoretical reconstruction

**Jörg Friedrichs, "The Meaning of New Medievalism," *EJIR* 7:4
(2001).** *[T2 — IN REPO, read first-hand]* The analytical engine: the
duality of competing universalistic claims (Empire/Church →
state-system/transnational market), definition at p. 475, redefinition
p. 482, restated p. 490; the disanalogy at p. 497. The book's Part I
correction — the second universalism institutionalised as churches, not
a market — is made against this text from its own pages. Engaged: Ch1
(central), Ch2 (the foil), Ch11–12.

**Philip G. Cerny, "Neomedievalism, Civil War and the New Security
Dilemma," *Civil Wars* 1:1 (1998).** *[T2 — IN REPO, read first-hand]*
The six-characteristic checklist (section headings, pp. 49–57; *zones
grises* after Minc, n. 16); "durable disorder"; the absent exogenous
pressure named as "the inter-dynastic struggles of the Hundred Years'
War" (p. 52); the prediction of "quasi-public, quasi-private dispute
settlement regimes" (p. 52); and the location of the first
consolidation in "the creative destruction of fifteenth to seventeenth
century warfare" (pp. 51–52, 58–59) — the sceptics-concede thread's
theoretical instance. Engaged: Ch1 (the checklist run), Ch8 (the sixth
characteristic), Ch10 (the mechanism attested), Ch12, Appendix C.

**Philip G. Cerny, "Terrorism and the New Security Dilemma," *NWCR*
58:1 (2005).** *[T2 — bundled]* The war-college-tested five-fold
restatement; working text pending the 1998 original's retrieval, now
superseded for enumeration by the original (in repo). Engaged: Ch1,
Ch3, Ch5, Ch8.

**Stephen J. Kobrin, "Back to the Future: Neomedievalism and the
Postmodern Digital World Economy," *JIA* 51:2 (1998).** *[T2 —
bundled]* The platforms line's origin: digital economies dissolve the
territorial basis of jurisdiction; overlapping, heteronomous authority
returns; and Kobrin's own anticipated *re-centralisation*, which the
book redirects from the supranational to the intra-bloc terminus.
Engaged: Ch2 (central), Ch4, Ch11–12.

## III. Security and private force

**Sean McFate, *Durable Disorder* (LSE thesis, 2011; *The Modern
Mercenary*, 2014).** *[T2 — thesis bundled; monograph on acquisition
list]* The eroded mercenary taboo as Bull's third trend returned; the
monopsony disanalogy (the US as consumer-in-chief; modern PMSCs without
independent fiscal bases) that Ch3 documents from SEC filings and Ch4
turns on. Engaged: Ch3 (central), Ch4, Ch11–12.

**Phil Williams, *From the New Middle Ages to a New Dark Age* (SSI,
2008).** *[T3 — bundled]* The pessimist counter-read: durable disorder
as a way-station that can tip; "alternatively governed" zones. Staged
against McFate by the book (chronology declared). Engaged: Ch3, Ch5,
Ch11–12.

## IV. European and regional application

**Ole Wæver, "European Security Identities," *JCMS* 34:1 (1996).**
*[T2 — bundled]* The securitisation lens and Wæver's own "new middle
ages" of concentric circles — the EU as post-sovereign system in a
security theorist's terms. Engaged: Ch11 (the EU church), Ch12.

**Jan Zielonka, *Europe as Empire* (2006).** *[T2 — GATED, acquisition
list]* The EU as neomedieval empire; needed for Ch2's EU-church section
at the upgrade pass.

## V. Popular and strategic synthesis

**Anne-Marie Slaughter, *A New World Order* (2004), chs. 5–6.** *[T2 —
bundled]* The optimist's read, engaged as important and wrong: networks
as the interregnum's connective tissue mistaken for architecture; the
capacity test applied in Ch1 §V. Engaged: Ch1, Ch2, Ch12.

**Robert D. Kaplan, "The Coming Anarchy" (1994).** *[T3 — bundled]* The
atmospheric predecessor and the declinism-cycle's finest instance;
quoted exactly, with the stretch-limo image credited to Homer-Dixon.
Engaged: Introduction (the first inoculation), Ch1.

**Parag Khanna, "The Next Big Thing: Neomedievalism" (*FP*, 2009).**
*[T3 — defective capture; acquisition list]* The mass-audience marker;
[ANALOGY-ONLY] per doctrine; annotation provisional pending a clean
copy.

## VI. The critics

**Bruce Holsinger, *Neomedievalism, Neoconservatism, and the War on
Terror* (2007).** *[T2 — punctum-carried page cites; re-verify on
purchase]* The medievalist's indictment: neomedievalism as "the global
idiom of the non-state actor" and a co-optable theoretical fashion.
Engaged: Introduction (the fairyland inoculation).

**Nicholas Rengger, the "fairyland" chapter.** *[T2 — GATED; only "its
calamitous predecessor" carried openly]* Not paraphrased beyond the
carried fragment until retrieved. Engaged: Introduction.

## VII. The historiographical anchor the literature lacked

**J. R. Green, *A Short History of the English People* (1874), ch. VI.**
*[T1 — IN REPO, public domain]* The "New Monarchy" coinage at source
("If we use the name of the New Monarchy to express the character of
the English sovereignty from the time of Edward the Fourth to the time
of Elizabeth…") — the contested frame Ch11 declares and extends.

# Appendix C — Leading Indicators

## How to score this book

The wager (Introduction, §III) fails if the indicators below move
against it in the directions stated, over the fifteen-year horizon from
publication. Two framing devices precede the numbered indicators.

**The context variable (formerly C1-1): exogenous territorialising
pressure.** Cerny's absent variable, returned. Baseline (2025–26): the
NATO Hague pledge of 24–25 June 2025 ("invest 5% of GDP annually… by
2035"); ReArm Europe's €800bn envelope (4 March 2025); China's 15th
Five-Year Plan self-reliance headline (March 2026); and a great-power
war on the record (CRS R48887, March 2026). Direction: the book expects
this pressure sustained; a lapse to post-Cold-War absence weakens every
indicator below.

**C11-1 — The five-move scorecard (the assembly table).** Each bloc
(US, China, EU, India) scored annually on the five moves: fiscal
absorption of platform surplus; subordination of great subjects
(enforced and upheld); domestication of private force; canonical
assertion (enforced, not merely enacted); communications/legibility
mobilisation. Baseline (mid-2026): the ch11 §VI record — US 4/5 moves
in execution (fiscal absorption partial), China 5/5 in execution, EU
2/5 enforced (canon enacted but delayed; no fisc), India 2/5
(legibility and fiscal; military-industrial lagging). Direction: the
wager's condition 4 fails the book if the scorecard stalls across all
four candidates.

---

## The indicators

**C10-2 — Leading-node concentration ratio** *(absorbs C6-1)*.
Measures: polities/jurisdictions able independently to field the
decisive layer (leading-node fabrication; proliferated military LEO;
hyperscale sovereign compute; peer-war munitions base). Baseline: 100%
of sub-10nm capacity in Taiwan (92%) and South Korea (8%) (SIA/BCG,
April 2021); one EUV supplier on earth, none shipped to China; two
polities flying proliferated military-relevant LEO (mid-2026).
Direction: count holds at ≤4 → thesis holds; rises materially above
four → wager condition 1 fails the book.

**C10-1 — Public financing of the military-relevant stack** *(absorbs
C6-2)*. Measures: central-budget lines for the stack, and their form
(subsidy → equity → ownership). Baseline: CHIPS $52.7bn appropriated
(Pub. L. 117-167, 9 Aug 2022) with the $8.9bn grant-to-equity
conversion (~10% of Intel, 22 Aug 2025); Big Fund III RMB 344bn (May
2024); EU Chips Act with ~€4.5bn directly Commission-controlled and
Magdeburg cancelled (July 2025). Direction: rising public share and
escalating form (toward equity/ownership) → thesis holds.

**C10-3 — Decisive-layer delivery gap** *(incorporates C5-1)*.
Measures: stated capability targets vs audited actuals. Baseline:
155mm at ~36k/month actual (DoD IG, March 2026) against the 100k
goal; average major-system fielding >12 years, lead frigate 3+ years
late (GAO); NDIS (Jan 2024): the base "does not possess the
capacity… required." Direction: a closing gap → consolidation
executing; a chronically open gap → the artillery-state response
failing in the probable case — scored against the US candidacy, not
the mechanism.

**C12-1 — Patron-dependence of the distributed layer** *(replaces
C6-4)*. Measures: whether cheap distributed systems operate without a
patron's intelligence, connectivity and interceptor layers. Baseline:
the March 2025 suspension week (aid 3rd; intelligence ~5th; imagery
8th; restoration 11th); ~42,000 Starlink terminals as the backbone;
Patriot stock at ~25% of planning requirement (July 2025). Direction:
a full-stack power defeated by unpatroned distributed systems fails
wager condition 1.

**C12-2 — Industrialisation of the cheap layer.** Measures: whether
the distributed layer's scaling re-concentrates into state industrial
policy. Baseline: Ukraine 2.2M drones produced 2024 (Zelensky, Feb
2025), ~3M FPVs delivered 2025 (Shmyhal, Dec 2025); Russian Shahed
launches from ~200/week to >1,000/week by March 2025 (CSIS); the
805-drone night of 6–7 Sept 2025 (Ukrainian Air Force). Direction:
continued industrialisation → the "cheap" layer confirms, rather than
refutes, the fiscal-military pattern.

**C2-1 — Nationalisation-of-function index** *(pairs with C4-1)*.
Measures: state absorption of platform functions (identity, payments,
compute, orbital, model provision) into public or publicly-controlled
instruments. Baseline (2025–26): Starshield ("owned and controlled by
the US government"); the GSA $1 agency AI deals (Aug 2025); e-CNY
operations centre; WAICO signed by 29 states (16 July 2026); the
Private Economy Promotion Law sequence (May 2025). Direction: rising
→ the Henrician settlement executing.

**C4-1 — Absorption-mode index.** Measures: *how* each bloc absorbs —
procurement/personnel vs regulation vs equity vs fusion. Baseline: US
procurement-and-personnel (programmes of record; Detachment 201, 13
June 2025; verified absence of golden shares in Palantir/Anduril —
against the Intel equity exception); EU canonical; China fused; India
sovereign-stack. Direction: convergence toward harder forms (equity,
fusion) → acceleration; scored per bloc.

**C2-3 — Platform-independence counter-indicator** *(the falsifier;
pairs with C3-2)*. Measures: an actor combining decisive-relevant
capability with a majority-commercial fiscal base that escapes state
instruments *and resolves disputes with states in fora states do not
control*. Baseline: zero instances — the strongest candidate episode
(Anthropic, 2026) was resolved in an Article III court, the state's
own forum, both parties bound (injunction 26 Mar 2026; restoration 3
Apr 2026). Direction: a genuine instance fails wager condition 3.

**C3-2 — Independent-fiscal-base emergence (the condottiere watch)**
*(refined by C4-2; context from C3-1)*. Measures: contractor-class
actors' revenue independence from the state paymaster. Baseline: the
conventional tier files 87–98% government revenue as a formal risk
(Booz Allen 98%, CACI 95.7%, Leidos ~87%, FY2023–25 10-Ks); the
frontier labs invert it (state ceilings ~$200M vs reported
multi-billion commercial run-rates, 2025–26, estimates flagged; a
$65bn raise during a federal blacklist, reported). Direction: the
inversion spreading beyond the labs, or a lab converting revenue into
recognised standing, moves condition 3's precursors.

**C7-1 — Populist-absorption index.** Measures: whether
propertied-anxious waves durably reduce the state's
fiscal-administrative reach (the ch07 falsifier). Baseline: the
post-Brexit UK tax take projected toward "a post-war high of 37.1% of
GDP by 2028-29" (OBR, March 2024); no gathered instance of durable
reach-reduction. Direction: a populist wave that durably shrinks
fiscal reach counts against the book.

**C8-2 — State reassertion over private fora** *(absorbs C8-1)*.
Measures: the tempo of state subordination of private/platform
adjudication. Baseline: Achmea (2018)/Komstroy (2021); ECT withdrawal
effective 27 June 2025; DSA Art. 21's non-binding cap; MiCA fully
applicable (Dec 2024); Binance $4,316,126,163 (21 Nov 2023); Google
remedies (Sept 2025). Direction: continued subordination-not-abolition
→ hierarchical plurality consolidating.

**C8-3 — Grey-zone legibility** *(absorbs C1-3)*. Measures: the
offshore stock and, decisively, its untaxed fraction. Baseline:
offshore household financial wealth ≈10% of world GDP; untaxed
fraction ~25%, down by a factor of ~3 since 2013; $12.6tn
CRS-reported in 2022 (EU Tax Observatory 2024). Direction: untaxed
fraction falling → legibility winning; rising again → wager condition
2 moves against the book.

**C11-2 — Bloc-internal vs cross-bloc fiscal reach.** Measures: the
divergence between within-bloc consolidation and between-bloc
harmonisation. Baseline: CRS/AEOI at 116 jurisdictions, 171M
accounts, ~€13tn (2024; OECD 2025) — against the Side-by-Side
carve-out taking US-parented groups outside the IIR/UTPR (applying 1
Jan 2026) and Canada's DST rescission (June 2025). Direction:
bloc-internal reach rising while cross-bloc instruments fragment →
the parallel-settlements geometry; bloc-internal reach *falling* →
condition 2 against the book.

**C11-3 — Court-check series.** Measures: instances of consolidating
states losing against their own subjects in their own courts — the
settlement's rule-of-law form, kept scoreable. Baseline (2025–26):
FTC v. Meta lost (18 Nov 2025, appealed); Apple contempt affirmed
against Apple (11 Dec 2025); GC upholds gatekeeper designation
against Apple (8 July 2026); the Anthropic injunction against the
state (26 Mar 2026). Direction: a continuing mixed series →
discipline-through-process (the book's reading); systematic state
defeat on the *core* moves → the settlement failing.

**C6-3 — Capitulation-as-procurement.** Measures: polities choosing
to buy/license/align rather than build the decisive layer — ch06's
surrender-without-siege as modern procurement. Baseline: two polities
field proliferated military LEO; the EU contracted to ~2030 (IRIS²,
16 Dec 2024); client-state patterns in chip and model licensing
(2025–26). Direction: alignments deepening → the cascade operating;
independent builds proliferating → scored with C10-2 against the
book.

# Appendix D — A Reader's Guide
# Appendix D — A Reader's Guide

*ASSEMBLED (first assembly, 2026-08-02). Routes through the book for
readers who arrive with different questions; the annotated literature
is Appendix A, the comparative register Appendix B, the scoreboard
Appendix C.*

**If you have one evening.** Read the Introduction, Chapter 6, Chapter
10 and Chapter 12. That is the spine: the wager, the repricing, the
machine, the prediction. Chapter 11 is the first addition if the
evening stretches.

**If you come from international relations.** Part I is written for
you, and its quarrel is with your shelf: Chapter 1 accepts and runs
the Bull–Friedrichs–Cerny apparatus; Chapter 2 amends Friedrichs's
second universalism; Chapter 12 §VI states what the book claims to
have added. Read the Coda for the precise disagreement with Bull
before deciding the book has misread him.

**If you come from medieval history.** The book's medieval claims are
deliberately narrower than its title suggests, and every chapter
states its debts and its gaps: Chapter 6 rests on Rogers, Curry,
DeVries and the chronicle editions, and its central mechanism
statement was built to reconcile the first two; Chapter 10 rests on
Contamine's monograph and articles at page level; the objection
stress-tests are in each chapter's critiques file, steelmanned first.
The place to attack is the mechanism's threshold step (Chapter 10
§VII) and the falsifiability passage's condition 1; the book would
rather be corrected at page level than admired in general.

**If you come from policy or strategy.** Chapters 5, 10, 11 and 12
are the working sequence: the failure modes of executive capacity;
the mechanism; the five moves with their current instruments, every
claim dated; the candidates and the military-technology answer.
Appendix C is the standing scoreboard. Nothing in Chapter 12 §VII is
investment guidance, by doctrine as well as disclaimer.

**If you come from the technology industry.** Read Chapter 2 to see
your institutions described functionally, Chapter 3 §V and Chapter 4
§V for the absorption pattern operating on your contracts and your
colleagues' commissions, Chapter 9 for the two-body problem in your
charters, and Chapter 11 §VII for the settlement the book expects —
operative but never invested. The Sforza case (Chapter 4 §III) is
the précis: it is about you.

**If you read for the history of ideas.** The book's method is the
sceptics-concede thread (introduced in the Introduction's second
inoculation, executed at Chapters 6 §VII, 10 §VII, 11 §V, and
summarised at 12 §VI), the falsifiability discipline (Introduction
§III; the narrowing deltas in the Coda §IV), and the provenance rules
(Coda §III). The book stands or falls on whether those disciplines
were kept; the flags in the text — [GAP], [RE-CHECK AT PRESS] — are
deliberately left visible in the working drafts so the keeping can be
checked.

**A note on the bundled sources.** The open-access neomedievalism
dossier that seeded Part I (Cerny 2005, Kobrin, McFate's thesis,
Williams, Slaughter chs. 5–6, Kaplan, Wæver, Khanna) is indexed in
this appendix's folder; Appendix A grades it and records what has
since been superseded by first-hand retrievals.
